# Mode-diff debate, round 2: rebuttal and fix-verify

Same debate, same rules. Your round 1 produced five confirmed findings
and I applied all five. One of them I am contesting in part, with a
measurement, and I want you to check my measurement rather than concede
to it.

The tree has MOVED since round 1. New head is the commit
`apply diff debate round 1: refuse trailing dot destinations, harden two
oracles, narrow three record claims`. The mirror you are working in was
rebuilt at the same path from that head, so what you read now is the
fixed tree, not the one you reviewed.

## Finding 1: I am contesting the CONSEQUENCE, not the defect

You reported the trailing-dot bypass as reaching recursive deletion of
the source, having executed the construction prefix on both hosts with
`Remove-Item` replaced by a harmless interceptor.

I measured what your interceptor stood in for. On BOTH Windows PowerShell
5.1 and PowerShell 7, with a real directory and a real file inside it:

- `Test-Path` on the dotted path returns True.
- `Remove-Item -LiteralPath <dotted> -Recurse -Force` throws
  `PSArgumentException` and deletes NOTHING.
- `Remove-Item <dotted> -Recurse -Force`, the non-literal form, throws
  the same and deletes nothing.
- The directory and the file inside it both survive.

So the deletion call is REACHED and cannot delete. Your probe established
reachability; substituting the interceptor is exactly what made the
difference invisible to it. I think the round 1 wording claims more than
the evidence supports, in the same way finding 3 caught me doing.

**I am not disputing that the guard is broken.** The removal failure is
non-terminating, this script never sets `$ErrorActionPreference`, so the
build continues and constructs a mirror at a path naming the tree under
review. That is a real defect with a real consequence, and it is
pre-existing rather than introduced by this branch - the same class the
branch had already closed for `-ExtraInput` and left open on the
destructive path.

**Check this.** If you can produce a real deletion through that path on
either host, without an interceptor, say so and I will withdraw the
rebuttal. If you cannot, I want you to say that too, plainly.

## What I changed

**Finding 1.** A spelling guard now runs BEFORE the overlap comparison,
on BOTH operands, since both are user-supplied. Any path component ending
in a dot or a space is refused, exiting 2, with `.` and `..` excluded.
Two tests: `test_a_mirror_path_with_a_trailing_dot_is_refused`, which
also asserts the source survives, and
`test_a_repo_root_with_a_trailing_space_is_refused`.

**Finding 2.** Both weak oracles now assert positively. The
control-character test requires the WHOLE rendered name, so a doubled
prefix or a mangled remainder fails it. The category test requires the
whole escaped name. Both also assert `unknown` is absent, which is what
closes the fallback path you identified.

**Finding 3.** `BACKLOG.md` item 93 and `gate-results.md` now say only
that the slowdown did not reproduce in three runs, that the cause is
unknown, and that no candidate is favoured or excluded. The claims "item
90 did not close a gap" and "not a property of the tracked tree" are
gone.

**Finding 4.** The README now says "the reviews missed them;
implementation exposed them", and records that an earlier draft claimed
impossibility and that you struck it.

**Finding 5.** The stale trailing-newline comment is gone.

Gate after the fixes: 156 passed, 1 skipped under BOTH hosts; backlog
lint clean; the script still pure ASCII.

## What I want from round 2

1. **Verify each of the five fixes against the code, not against this
   summary.** A fix that does not do what its description says is worth
   more to me than a new finding.
2. **Adjudicate finding 1's consequence.** Named above.
3. **Attack the NEW guard specifically.** It is the newest code in the
   tree and nothing has reviewed it. Is the component split right? Does
   it refuse anything legitimate - a UNC path, a drive-relative path, a
   path with a trailing separator already stripped elsewhere? Can a
   spelling still reach the overlap comparison that names a directory
   other than what it compares as? I am thinking specifically about `..`
   segments, short 8.3 names, and alternate data stream syntax, none of
   which I checked.
4. **Continue the class sweep.** Same instruction as round 1: instances
   of the CLASSES, or an explicit none, plus the shapes you searched.
   Negative-only oracles are now a known class in this tree - are there
   others beyond the two you found?

## Output

Same format. Numbered findings, CONFIRMED or SUSPECTED, file and line for
every claim, and a verdict of PASS, FIX or ESCALATE with one sentence on
what it rests on. If the fixes hold and the sweep is empty, PASS is the
honest answer and I would rather have it than a manufactured finding.

+def test_a_mirror_path_with_a_trailing_dot_is_refused(tmp_path):
+    """The overlap guard compares SPELLING, and Windows strips a trailing
+    dot when it OPENS a path, so `<repo>.` is a different string naming
+    the same directory. It passes the equal, inside and contains tests
+    and reaches the build.
+
+    Measured 2026-09-05 under BOTH hosts before this guard existed: the
+    dotted path passes `Test-Path`, and `Remove-Item` on it throws
+    `PSArgumentException` and deletes NOTHING, on `-LiteralPath` and on
+    `-Path` alike. So the consequence is NOT the recursive deletion the
+    round-1 reviewer reported reaching; the deletion cannot run. What
+    survives the bypass is worse-shaped than it looks anyway: the failed
+    removal is non-terminating, the build continues, and it proceeds to
+    construct a mirror whose path names the repository under review.
+
+    Refuse the spelling this tool cannot resolve, exactly as the extra
+    input guard already does for the same reason.
+    """
+    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
+    proc = run_mirror(repo, pathlib.Path(str(repo) + "."), "-Force")
+    assert proc.returncode == 2, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
+    assert "dot or a space" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout
+    assert (repo / "kept.txt").exists(), "the source must survive a refusal"
+
+
+def test_a_repo_root_with_a_trailing_space_is_refused(tmp_path):
+    """The same defect on the other side of the comparison. Both
+    operands are user-supplied, so guarding one of them leaves the class
+    open."""
+    repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
+    proc = run_mirror(pathlib.Path(str(repo) + " "), tmp_path / "mirror")
+    assert proc.returncode == 2, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
+    assert "dot or a space" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout
+

+# SPELLING GUARD, and it runs before the overlap guard because the
+# overlap guard is a STRING COMPARISON and this is the input that defeats
+# one. Windows strips a trailing dot or space when it OPENS a path;
+# PowerShell keeps it in the string. So `<repo>.` names the repository and
+# compares unequal to it, passing the equal, inside and contains tests
+# below.
+#
+# Measured 2026-09-05 on BOTH hosts, because the round-1 reviewer reported
+# this as reaching recursive deletion of the source and that part is not
+# what happens: the dotted path passes `Test-Path`, and `Remove-Item`
+# throws `PSArgumentException` and removes NOTHING, on `-LiteralPath` and
+# `-Path` alike. The removal failure is NON-TERMINATING though, and this
+# script does not set `$ErrorActionPreference`, so the build would carry
+# on and construct a mirror at a path naming the tree under review. Refuse
+# the spelling rather than reason about what each downstream call does
+# with it - the same decision, for the same reason, as the -ExtraInput
+# guard further down.
+foreach ($pair in @(@("the repo root", $RepoRoot), @("the mirror path", $MirrorPath))) {
+    foreach ($seg in ([string]$pair[1]).Replace("\", "/").Split("/")) {
+        if ($seg -match '[. ]$' -and $seg -ne "." -and $seg -ne "..") {
+            Write-Output ("ERROR: " + $pair[0] + " (" + $pair[1] + ") has a" +
+                " path component ending in a dot or a space, which Windows" +
+                " strips when it opens the path but PowerShell keeps in the" +
+                " string, so this tool cannot tell which directory it names;" +
+                " pass the exact name")
+            exit 2
+        }
+    }

1282: # on and construct a mirror at a path naming the tree under review. Refuse
1283: # the spelling rather than reason about what each downstream call does
1284: # with it - the same decision, for the same reason, as the -ExtraInput
1285: # guard further down.
1286: foreach ($pair in @(@("the repo root", $RepoRoot), @("the mirror path", $MirrorPath))) {
1287:     foreach ($seg in ([string]$pair[1]).Replace("\", "/").Split("/")) {
1288:         if ($seg -match '[. ]$' -and $seg -ne "." -and $seg -ne "..") {
1289:             Write-Output ("ERROR: " + $pair[0] + " (" + $pair[1] + ") has a" +
1290:                 " path component ending in a dot or a space, which Windows" +
1291:                 " strips when it opens the path but PowerShell keeps in the" +
1292:                 " string, so this tool cannot tell which directory it names;" +
1293:                 " pass the exact name")
1294:             exit 2
1295:         }
1296:     }
1297: }
1298: 
1299: # OVERLAP GUARD, before anything is created or deleted. -Force recursively
1300: # deletes MirrorPath, so a MirrorPath equal to, inside, or containing
1301: # RepoRoot would destroy the user's working tree. robocopy over an
1302: # overlapping pair is equally unsafe. This runs FIRST among the
1303: # comparisons, because by the time Remove-Item runs it is too late to
1304: # check.
1305: $rr = $RepoRoot.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/") + "/"
1306: $mp = $MirrorPath.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/") + "/"
1307: $cmp = [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase
1308: if ($mp.Equals($rr, $cmp)) {
1309:     Write-Output "ERROR: the mirror path is the repo root itself"
1310:     exit 2
1311: }
1312: if ($mp.StartsWith($rr, $cmp)) {
1313:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the mirror path is inside the repo ($MirrorPath)" +
1314:         " - building or forcing there would write into, or delete, the" +
1315:         " tree under review")
1316:     exit 2
1317: }
1318: if ($rr.StartsWith($mp, $cmp)) {
1319:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the mirror path contains the repo ($MirrorPath)" +
1320:         " - -Force would delete the repo with it")
1321:     exit 2
1322: }
1323: 
1324: # Resolve the EFFECTIVE override path here, default included, and guard it
1325: # beside the mirror guard. Deferring the default until after the mirror is
1326: # built, copied, remediated and manifested would mean discovering a stale
1327: # or overlapping artifact only after all that work had already happened,
1328: # and -SkipProbe would bypass the check entirely.
1329: if (-not $OverrideOut) {
1330:     $OverrideOut = Join-Path (Split-Path $MirrorPath -Parent) `
1331:         ((Split-Path $MirrorPath -Leaf) + ".skills-override.txt")
1332: }
1333: $op = $OverrideOut.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/")
1334: foreach ($protected in @($rr, $mp)) {
1335:     if (($op + "/").Equals($protected, $cmp) -or
1336:         ($op + "/").StartsWith($protected, $cmp) -or
1337:         $protected.StartsWith($op + "/", $cmp)) {
1338:         Write-Output ("ERROR: the override path overlaps a protected tree" +
1339:             " ($OverrideOut)")
1340:         exit 2
1341:     }
1342: }
1343: if (Test-Path $OverrideOut) {
1344:     Write-Output ("ERROR: $OverrideOut already exists - a stale override" +
1345:         " reads exactly like a fresh one")
1346:     exit 2
1347: }
1348: 
1349: # THE ADVISORY SOURCE MANIFEST'S DESTINATION, resolved and LEXICALLY
1350: # guarded here, beside the override, for the same stated reason: a
1351: # destination discovered after the build has copied, remediated and
1352: # manifested is discovered too late, and -SkipProbe would bypass a check
1353: # placed later. Its ALIAS guards are further down with the override's,
1354: # and its removal is after both, because a build that is going to be
1355: # refused must not have deleted anything first.
1356: $smResult = Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath $MirrorPath
1357: if ($smResult.Kind -eq "root") {
1358:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the mirror path is a filesystem root" +
1359:         " ($MirrorPath), which has no sibling, so no advisory source" +
1360:         " manifest can be placed beside it")
1361:     exit 2
1362: }
1363: if ($smResult.Kind -ne "ok") {
1364:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the mirror path could not be resolved in order to" +
1365:         " place an advisory source manifest beside it ($MirrorPath): " +
1366:         $smResult.Reason)
1367:     exit 2
1368: }
1369: $SourceManifestOut = $smResult.Path
1370: $smp = $SourceManifestOut.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/")
1371: foreach ($protected in @($rr, $mp)) {
1372:     if (($smp + "/").Equals($protected, $cmp) -or
1373:         ($smp + "/").StartsWith($protected, $cmp) -or
1374:         $protected.StartsWith($smp + "/", $cmp)) {
1375:         Write-Output ("ERROR: the source manifest path overlaps a protected" +
1376:             " tree ($SourceManifestOut)")
1377:         exit 2
1378:     }
1379: }
1380: if ($smp.Equals($op, $cmp)) {
1381:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the source manifest path is the override path" +
1382:         " ($SourceManifestOut) - the advisory write would replace the file" +
1383:         " the probe verified and the wrapper hashes")
1384:     exit 2
1385: }
1386: if ($SourceManifestOut.Length -ge 260) {
1387:     Write-Output ("ERROR: path budget exceeded by the source manifest - " +
1388:         "$SourceManifestOut is $($SourceManifestOut.Length) characters " +
1389:         "and the limit is 260")
1390:     exit 2
1391: }
1392: 
1393: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
1394: # PATH BUDGET PRE-FLIGHT. Runs here, after the overlap and override
1395: # guards and BEFORE anything is created or deleted, because the failure
1396: # it prevents is a MID-COPY one: robocopy stops at the first destination
1397: # it cannot create, leaving a partially populated mirror that reads
1398: # exactly like a complete one.
1399: #
1400: # THE UNIVERSE, frozen text: every file and directory destination that
1401: # the exact `robocopy /E` operation may create beneath the resolved
1402: # mirror root, including tracked, untracked, ignored, and all `.git`
1403: # content. Two consequences that narrower readings get wrong - a
1404: # directory with no files in it is still a destination, and `.git` is
1405: # copied so `.git` counts.
1406: #
1407: # THE LIMIT is 260 characters as a conservative policy across both
1408: # supported PowerShell hosts. It is a deterministic refusal threshold,
1409: # not a claim about the maximum any host, API, OS configuration, or
1410: # downstream client could support.
1780: # a dangling FILE SYMLINK was not measured at all, so nothing here claims
1781: # anything about one.
1782: $smAttr = $null
1783: try {
1784:     $smAttr = [System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($SourceManifestOut)
1785: } catch [System.IO.FileNotFoundException] {
1786:     $smAttr = $null
1787: } catch [System.IO.DirectoryNotFoundException] {
1788:     $smAttr = $null
1789: } catch {
1790:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the source manifest path could not be examined" +
1791:         " ($SourceManifestOut): " + $_.Exception.Message)
1792:     exit 2
1793: }
1794: if ($null -ne $smAttr) {
1795:     if (([int]$smAttr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -ne 0) {
1796:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $SourceManifestOut is a directory - this tool" +
1797:             " replaces a file there and never removes a tree")
1798:         exit 2
1799:     }
1800:     if (-not $Force) {
1801:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $SourceManifestOut already exists - pass" +
1802:             " -Force to replace it, the same rule the mirror path follows")
1803:         exit 2
1804:     }
1805:     # REMOVING a link removes the link and never its target's bytes,
1806:     # which is exactly why the removal is safe where a write through it
1807:     # was not.
1808:     Remove-Item -LiteralPath $SourceManifestOut -Force
1809: }
1810: 
1811: if (Test-Path $MirrorPath) {
1812:     if (-not $Force) {
1813:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $MirrorPath already exists - a stale mirror" +
1814:             " reads exactly like a fresh one. Pass -Force to replace it.")
1815:         exit 2
1816:     }
1817:     Remove-Item -LiteralPath $MirrorPath -Recurse -Force
1818: }
1819: New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $MirrorPath | Out-Null
1820: $MirrorPath = (Resolve-Path $MirrorPath).Path
1821: 
1822: # CONSTRUCTION BRIDGE, step 1: capture the source identity BEFORE the
1823: # copy. Everything after this compares against this value, not against a
1824: # fresh read, because a fresh read cannot tell a stable source from one
1825: # that moved and moved back.
1826: $sourceBefore = Get-HeadSha $RepoRoot
1827: if (-not $sourceBefore.Ok) {
1828:     Write-Output ("BLOCKED: " + $sourceBefore.Reason + " - the mirror's" +
1829:         " identity in the debate record would be blank on the source side")
1830:     exit 1

96: def run_mirror(repo, mirror, *extra, env=None):
97:     """`env` carries the build's three TEST SEAMS.
98: 
99:     They are environment variables, not parameters, for the reason the
100:     lane-home builder states about its own two: a parameter is public
101:     surface on a shipped tool. No shipped caller sets either.
102: 
103:     Each is a BOOLEAN that ORs one extra block condition into a
104:     comparison. It cannot supply a value, so it cannot suppress a real
105:     measurement, and setting it can only ever ADD a reason to fail.
106:     That is what makes the one-way claim structural rather than a
107:     property of the value someone happens to pass.
108: 
109:     Two earlier shapes failed that bar and the whole-branch review
110:     caught both. The first copied from a different tree and committed
111:     into the real source repo. The second INJECTED a value, so a parent
112:     that set the variable to the repo's own current HEAD - trivially
113:     readable in advance - suppressed the genuine post-copy measurement
114:     and turned a build that should have failed into one that passed.
115:     """
116:     full = None
117:     if env:
118:         full = dict(os.environ)
119:         full.update(env)
120:     return subprocess.run(
121:         [ps_host(), "-NoProfile", "-NonInteractive", "-File", str(MIRROR),
122:          "-RepoRoot", str(repo), "-MirrorPath", str(mirror),
123:          "-SkipProbe", *extra],
124:         capture_output=True, text=True, env=full)
125: 
126: 
127: SKIP_BLOCK = "BLOCKED: no client measurement was made (-SkipProbe)"
128: 
129: 
130: def assert_built(proc):
131:     """-SkipProbe builds the mirror but VERIFIES nothing, so it exits 1.
132: 
133:     The exit code reports dispatch-readiness, not construction: a mirror
134:     with no client measurement is not cleared for dispatch, and must not
135:     share an exit code with one that is. The mode-diff review of
136:     2026-07-28 found the old exit 0 there.
137: 
138:     Asserting the SPECIFIC skip line is what keeps these tests able to
139:     catch a real construction failure, which also exits 1 but with a
140:     different reason.
141:     """
142:     assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
143:     assert SKIP_BLOCK in proc.stdout, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
144:

146: def make_junction(link, target):
147:     """A junction via mklink /J, or skip. Junctions need no privilege;
148:     symbolic links do, and this session cannot make one."""
149:     rc = subprocess.run(["cmd", "/c", "mklink", "/J", str(link), str(target)],
150:                         capture_output=True, text=True)
151:     if rc.returncode != 0:
152:         pytest.skip("junction creation unavailable: " + rc.stderr)
153: 
154: 
155: def unlink_junction(link):
156:     """rmdir removes the junction and never its target."""
157:     subprocess.run(["cmd", "/c", "rmdir", str(link)],
158:                    capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
159: 
160: 
161: def is_reparse_point(path):
162:     """Python's is_symlink() is False for a junction, so read the
163:     attribute bit the filesystem sets for both kinds of link."""
164:     return bool(os.lstat(str(path)).st_file_attributes
165:                 & stat.FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT)

168: def read_block(stdout, label):
169:     """Lines of one labelled block from the record output."""
170:     lines = stdout.splitlines()
171:     start = next(i for i, line in enumerate(lines) if line.startswith(label))
172:     out = []
173:     for line in lines[start + 1:]:
174:         if line and not line.startswith("  "):
175:             break
176:         if line.strip():
177:             out.append(line.strip())
178:     return out

The stream-form source case reaches deletion with the ordinary, undotted source pathname as the destination. ([deletion call](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1817))

   The guard enumerates only source and mirror. Override protection still compares spelling and checks for reparse points.    MirrorPath: C:\Temp\round2-destination-probe

   **Change:** apply the same spelling/identity validation to the effective override destination before its overlap check, with a source-survival regression.
